### Journey verification LIX (2026-09-12, MVP finish-line program — the library floor reported a crate red that is green, and the truth was only in the annotation)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **An innocent 3,635-test suite wore a runner's disk exhaustion.** The library-suite floor failed two steps: `ioi-networking`, the expected fence on the frozen AFT surface, and `ioi-services`, which the floor lists as expected-green and which is fenced nowhere. Measured before concluding anything: that suite is GREEN on the qualification host at the same commit — 3635 passed, 0 failed, 4 ignored, 368 s. The CI step ran 35m57s before failing, a 5.9x factor sitting exactly inside the 5–8x runner band this estate has already measured, so the comfortable hypothesis was another real-clock timing defect of the kind already recorded against the networking crate. That hypothesis is wrong. | the job annotation: `System.IO.IOException: No space left on device`, raised inside the runner's own worker | the crate is not a defect; no crate fix is owed |
| **A disk exhaustion is reported against whichever step was running when the volume filled, which makes it a slander generator.** It is not a test failure and not a timeout — the job used 117 of its 150 minutes — so nothing in the step's own output says what happened. The only place the cause is written is the job annotation, which is not in the step log and not in any test output. A reader following the red to the crate finds 3,635 passing tests and no explanation. | the step timings against the local run | recorded as the finding that matters |
| **The cause is structural, and it is this program's own change from earlier the same day.** The floor compiles the TEST TARGET of twenty-five crates into one cached `target` on a standard runner with roughly 14 GB free. Splitting the floor into one step per crate — landed hours earlier to end a masking defect where the first red aborted the block and seventeen crates below it never ran — is precisely what made the job reach crates it had never compiled before. The unmasking worked; the first thing it exposed was the job's own disk budget. There was no disk-freeing step anywhere in the workflow. | `ci.yml` before this cut | fixed where the defect is |
| **The floor now frees disk as part of its design and states its headroom three times.** `df -h /` prints before the cleanup, after it, and again after the cache restore — which is where the target directory actually lands — so the next exhaustion names itself in the job's own output rather than crashing the worker and accusing a crate. | the added step | fixed; closure test is the next run reporting `ioi-services` green with headroom printed |

### Journey verification LVIII (2026-09-12, MVP finish-line program, leg 1 — M07.1: the unit was blocked on a measurement that searched for a word instead of an object, and one real obligation was left when that was corrected)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **A registered, generated, implemented contract was recorded as absent, because the search was for a WORD.** The unit was ruled a blocker partly on "`entitlement` is a ZERO-hit token repo-wide" and "no registered contract exists for a credit or a budget". Both are false: `managed-work-billing-ledger-bundle.v1` is registered, generated into Rust types and implemented in `economics_routes.rs`, and its `plan` definition IS the commercial plan/entitlement object — `plan_ref`, `version`, `body_hash`, `rate_card_ref`, `rate_card_body_hash`, `included_work_credits`, a `reset_policy` of `non_resetting / monthly_expiring / contract_term_expiring`, and an exact validity window, with `quote` binding it by ref AND by body hash. The word "entitlement" appears nowhere precisely because the estate spells the entitlement `included_work_credits`. This is the same error class as deriving a behaviour population by grepping a field name, now earned a third time. | private register R-70; the contract, its generated type and its daemon implementation | corrected before any check was authored against the wrong picture |
| **Six of the seven obligations already had subjects, and establishing that is what made the seventh visible.** Supplier statements (`usage_record.supplier_statement_refs`, `cost_breakdown.supplier_reconciliation_state`, a bundle-level `assurance_status`), duplicate/stale heads (`sequence` + `previous_usage_hash`, `usage_head_hash` on both the overrun decision and the final debit, `previous_adjustment_hash`, `ledger_head_hash`, and `hold.idempotency_key` for same-key replay), overrun (`exact_overage_work_credits` under a declared `block`/`exact_additional_hold` policy), adjustment (`refund`/`writeoff` with mandatory evidence and a ceiling at its debit), and non-transfer as a pinned absence were all present. The arithmetic is integer micro-credits, overflow REFUSES rather than wraps, every usage record must cite a runtime receipt, and a coarse OCU projection riding a billable meter is a typed refusal at admission — so the "reproduce from receipts" half of ACC-11 clause 9 was built, not pending. | the module's enforced refusal vocabulary | measured |
| **The one genuine absence: one owner had exactly ONE billing account, so network/open work could not draw a separate budget.** `billing_account_ref` was derived from `caller.owner_ref` alone. The registered `charge_component` enum already draws the line the budget needs — `broker`, `participant`, `verifier` against `managed_model`, `managed_runtime`, `ioi_managed_service` — so the partition uses the estate's own vocabulary rather than a second classification that could disagree with it. **The separation is STRUCTURAL rather than arithmetic:** one quote binds one rate card and resolves one account, so a card pricing both classes could only ever draw one budget for both; such a card is refused at ADMISSION, and the account is scoped by the class the bound card's own meters resolve to, never by the caller. `non_billable_telemetry` is in neither class, so a zero-rate coarse meter cannot move work between budgets. | nine daemon tests green; two planted defects each turned exactly one test red and the failure NAMED the assertion | built |
| **The gate asserts the partition as an ENTAILMENT over the registered vocabulary, not as today's list.** It reads the daemon's own `CHARGE_COMPONENTS` constant, cross-checks it against the registered contract's enum, and requires the two classes to cover the billable vocabulary exactly and disjointly — so a charge component added tomorrow and left unclassified is a finding tomorrow rather than a silent default into the managed budget. A gate listing today's six could not do that. | `check:work-credit-budget-contract` 23/23 with six mutation drills | authored; the unit closes |
| **Two of the gate's OWN assertions were wrong first, and both failure modes are reusable.** It stripped string literals and then searched for refusal codes that ARE string literals — six assertions red for a reason with nothing to do with the product — and a naive literal regex also mis-paired across Rust raw strings and corrupted the text it was reading. Separately the non-transferability pin counted word occurrences, so the module's own nonclaim prose ("no settlement, escrow, payout, or exchange execution lives here") tripped it, while a daemon-wide route scan reported an OAuth `portal-session-exchange` as a credit conversion. The pin now reads function DEFINITIONS and this plane's own routes, and a drill asserts the nonclaim prose does not trip it. A gate that fails for its own reasons is not evidence about its subject. | the gate's own first run, recorded as it happened | fixed in the same commit as the gate |
| **A stale acceptance reference beside the unit, found while closing it.** The module doc cited `ACC-11` clause 1 where the manifest has always cited clause 9; clause 9 is the invoice-grade metering clause that names the separate network/open budget, and clause 1 is a different subject. Corrected, with the old value recorded in place so the change is legible. | the journey document's own clause list | fixed |

### Journey verification LVII (2026-09-12, MVP finish-line program, leg 0 — the product plane leaves the MVP by cutting six real dependencies, not by flipping a flag)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **A product plane sat in the release graph by DEFAULT, and six MVP units held it there.** Canon is layered and unambiguous — ADR 0022 places GoalRun and OutcomeRoom in the ioi.ai application domain, ADR 0030 removed the room family's parallel admission/receipt/sequence/root spine, ADR 0031 rules that GoalRun execution COMPOSES five daemon-owned primitives and owns no second orchestration spine, ADR 0052 Decision 4 moved their contracts into `domains/ioi-ai/` — yet the manifest carried M04.7, M04.8, M12.4 and ACC-9 as `mvp_required` by the manifest's DEFAULT, none by a recorded decision, while ACC-19 over the same product was explicitly `product_track`. The scope flags could not move because the structure checker forbids an MVP unit depending on a non-MVP one, and six such edges existed. | the manifest's `program_scope_default`; the checker's cross-scope edge rule | ruled R-68; the six edges cut, then the flags moved |
| **Every one of the six was a dependent reaching a PLATFORM guarantee THROUGH a FEATURE, so all six are rewires rather than deletions.** `M11.1 → M04.8` was the clearest: M04.8's own scope row explicitly LEAVES discovery publication, `ParticipantStateBundle` production/export and external participation to their declared owners, and M11.1 IS that owner — it never consumed M04.8, so it re-points to the generalized admitted-record contract M04.4 it actually needs. `M12.5 → M12.4` and `M12.6 → M12.4` were HORIZON ORDER, 1A before 1B before 2, not substrate: M12.4's own scope row says it covers "the boundary that a proof which only works for rooms has proved a room", and the substrate both were reaching through it for is the proof gate M12.3. `M04.12 → M04.7 and M04.8` re-points to M04.4 and M04.5, because M04.12's residue IS the no-second-spine guarantee applied to composition. | the units' scope rows, read before the edges were touched | fixed |
| **Moving M04.12 instead would have traded six violations for two, and only measuring the reverse edges showed it.** The obvious reading — M04.12's scope names the OutcomeRoom package, so move it with the rest — is wrong: M08.17 and M10.9 are both `mvp_required` and both depend on M04.12, so the move creates two fresh cross-scope violations. M04.12, M08.17 and M10.9 stay in the MVP; what leaves is the room package, the participation plane, the flagship demonstration and their journey. | the reverse-dependency enumeration over all 166 units | the direction of the cut decided by measurement, not by the scope prose |
| **The cut opened a silent hole, and the checker would never have said so.** M04.12 is `mvp_required` and its ONLY declared acceptance was ACC-9 — the journey this leg moves to `product_track` and that ACC-R consequently stops composing. There is no rule that an MVP unit's acceptance gate is itself MVP, so the tool stayed green through the re-scope and this would have shipped as a hole rather than a red. The same measurement cleared M08.17 (acceptance ACC-9 AND ACC-10) and M10.9 (ACC-12), which keep MVP homes; M04.12 was the only one. It re-points to ACC-5 — the M04 journey that already declares the module and whose clauses 4 and 5 are the two units M04.12 now depends on — which gains clause 10 for the guarantee, its negative clause, and `check:collective-artifact-runtime-lifecycle` as that clause's evidence. | R-69; the acceptance field of all 121 `mvp_required` units read once | fenced in the same commit as the cut that made it reachable |
| **The re-measurement, stated as the tool states it.** 68 → 67 blockers; 124 → 121 `mvp_required` units; 16 → 15 composed journeys; `check:release-readiness-scope` 6/6, with the excluded journeys exactly the six `product_track` gates. A scope cut moves what is COUNTED and never what is PROVED: the single retired blocker was ACC-9's unauthored journey check, which left the release graph together with its subject rather than becoming evidence. | `implementation-program.mjs --check` and `--release-readiness`; `check-release-readiness-scope.mjs` | leg 0 closed |

### Journey verification LVI (2026-09-12, MVP finish-line program — M07.3: the credential bought the figures, not the semantics)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The estate had one reconciliation route and it reconciled the estimate against itself.** Its own doc comment says so: reconciliation over existing records only, because actual provider bills are never invented. That is the estate correctly declining to fabricate a figure it cannot source, and it is why the gap was a gap rather than a bug. The acceptance asks for the other thing — a charge that may or may not have landed, reconciled against provider billing before any figure is reported. | a billing statement is now an admitted, owner-scoped, immutable object, read back through the caller's own scope rather than from the request | the readback exists and needed no credential to build |
| **The comparison is NOT total against total, and measuring first is what prevented building it that way.** A first cut assumed both sides had totals. The op path records a RATE and a ceiling per exposure and deliberately never computes a total, because until teardown there is not one — so a total-against-total comparison would have had to invent our side, the exact thing the old route refused to do. What is compared instead is coverage both ways plus the authorized ceiling of each CLOSED exposure, which is arithmetic from a start, an end and a rate this estate itself authorized. | full coverage within ceiling reconciles and only then is a figure reported; above ceiling diverges; a line naming an exposure never opened, an exposure the bill omits, and an exposure still open are each ambiguous | rebuilt against the measured record shape |
| **An open exposure is ambiguous by construction, which is the acceptance's own sentence as a state machine.** It is literally a charge that may or may not have landed, and reconciling a bill against a number that is still moving is the failure the clause names. A reported total exists in exactly one state. | the check exercises all three outcomes and both directions of coverage | the clause is enforced rather than described |
| **No retry before reconciliation, enforced where spending starts.** An unresolved reconciliation gates that provider's next create or redeploy, refused before the adapter resolves and before any credential is touched. The gate is per provider so one open question never freezes the estate, covers create and redeploy but never teardown — refusing a teardown because the bill is disputed would strand the resource still accruing the charge — and survives a restart because it is derived from admitted records rather than held in memory. | the check stops the daemon, restarts it on the same data directory, and finds the gate still shut | enforced, and restart is storage rather than a feature |
| **The admission census caught a real defect in the new module before anything was pinned.** The charge-gate projection's record id was written as an inlined string replacement, and the census reads the constant-shaped leaves of a write call's arguments to decide which family is being written — so the replacement string read to it as an unresolvable family leaf, and the run named it. The id is now a named binding, which is clearer code and leaves the write call's arguments as exactly what they are. | the census went from naming that function to four clean pin drifts, all attributable to one module joining | the gate found it; the pin is the bookkeeping after |

### Journey verification LV (2026-09-12, MVP finish-line program — M07.2: a contract cited by hash is not a contract applied)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **A rights contract was being cited as the basis of a compiled worker binding while its ceiling was never consulted.** The shared resolver takes no route-use argument — it resolves a contract — so per-use eligibility is structurally the caller's. Of three consumer planes, one enforced fully, one enforced a different axis deliberately and said so in its own comment, and the third called the liveness accessor and nothing else, then stamped the contract's revision and content hash into every compiled field contract it produced. Citing a contract by hash is evidence it was READ; it is not evidence its ceiling was OBEYED, and the two were being conflated. | found by measurement rather than by a failing test; fixed with three typed causes, because a contract that is not current, a right nobody has settled, and a use the contract affirmatively does not carry are three findings for three desks | fixed, with the check that proves it in the same commit |
| **The property took three corrections, and each wrong version was wrong in an instructive way.** Grepping the field names called a guarded plane unguarded, because that plane reaches its ceiling through an accessor. Requiring every resolving function to call a ceiling itself then accused two more, innocent for two DIFFERENT reasons: one resolves and never binds the result, its stated purpose being that the reference resolves under the caller's own owner binding; the other binds it and hands it to the fold that applies. Counting citations of the identity fields was no better, since those are ordinary field names on many objects in those modules. What separates the defect is what happens to the BOUND value. | two of the six drills exist solely to keep both innocents clean, because a rule that catches the defect by accusing the innocent is strict rather than right | the ceiling vocabulary is derived from the resolved type's own impl blocks, so it cannot drift from the type it describes |
| **The contract half is deliberately not restated.** The registered contract's closed twelve-use vocabulary, its pinned vocabulary size, the requirement that the unresolved lane be the exact projection of the unresolved findings, and the requirement that prohibitions be covered exactly by the affirmative uses plus those are all registered invariants the registry-driven gate already proves. | the new gate asserts the runtime obligation only | a second opinion on a settled question is not coverage |
| **A scope question had to be ruled before any of it counted.** The unit carries no explicit program scope, so it defaults to mvp-required, yet its acceptance names two journeys the manifest marks product-track and the program puts deliberately out. Ruled: the unit's obligation is the in-scope journey's share. A unit does not become product-track because its acceptance mentions one, and an mvp-required unit does not pull one back in by citation. | recorded in the gate's own nonclaims, not only in the private register | ruled, owner-reversible |

### Journey verification LIV (2026-09-12, MVP finish-line program — M06.4: refusing what is wrong is not the same as reproducing what is right)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The estate could refuse a tampered bundle and could describe a well-formed one, and neither says it reproduces a decision.** Six structural tests cover the bundle FORMAT — canonical hashing ignores key order, contracts exclude their self-hash field, a traversal filename is rejected, assembler output is exclusive, versioned identity resolves by committed hash, duplicate ref and hash binding stays forbidden. A separate battery rejects forty fully resealed semantic mutations, which is the INVERSE claim. A verifier that refused everything would satisfy both and reproduce nothing. | the bundle is now handed to the standalone offline relying party with no daemon and re-derives the same certificate hash twice running | the missing claim is now asserted |
| **"Reordering changes nothing" is true of one kind of order and false of another, and the first cut of the gate assumed wrong.** Key order inside a member is presentation: the same object serialised with its keys reversed verifies, with the decision and certificate hash unchanged, which is exactly what semantic continuity rather than blob presence means. The manifest's member ARRAY is not presentation: an array's order is part of its value, so reversing it is refused on the bundle hash — correctly, because a member list that could be rearranged after sealing would make the seal meaningless. | both measured, with opposite expected outcomes, and both asserted | a gate asserting either alone would have called a real binding a bug or called canonicalisation one |
| **A battery can pass because nothing works rather than because everything does.** The gate's first run omitted two arguments the offline verifier requires, so every acceptance failed on a usage error that read exactly like a rejection: the pristine replay went red and the exhaustive MISSING axis went GREEN for it, since everything was being refused. Recorded in the script beside the fix, because the shape is general — an axis that expects refusals is satisfied by a harness that cannot succeed. | the pristine run is now asserted first, and a drill re-runs the untouched bundle to prove the refusals come from the defect and not the harness | fixed, and the failure mode is named where it happened |
| **The fixture is composed, not copied.** Assembling a valid bundle is two hundred and fifty lines the relying-party gate already owns. A second copy would be a second drifting definition of what "valid" means — the defect this program corrected three times elsewhere in the same session, in workflow feature strings, in a per-surface stylesheet rule, and in a route-label proxy. That gate now emits its fixture on request and remains its only author. | the emitting gate re-run unchanged at forty rejections | one owner |

### Journey verification LIII (2026-09-12, MVP finish-line program — M01.7: a retained live proof is not a standing one)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Three gates judged the retained evidence's internal consistency and none asked whether it still described this tree.** The bounded owner-authorized live execution happened and its evidence is retained; the claim manifest, the assurance floor and the capstone bundle all prove things about it without contacting a provider. All three stay green on a tree the run never touched, because each judges the evidence against itself. The applicability question — does the commit that run was performed on resolve here and is it an ANCESTOR of HEAD — is the one property that decays silently, and a rebase, a squash or a clone from a different lineage breaks exactly it and nothing else. | measured before authoring: the integrated source commit IS an ancestor of HEAD; the benchmark commit resolves but is not, because that run was on its own branch and was never merged | the gap is closed as a gate, and both facts are asserted rather than assumed |
| **The unit asked for the committed certificate hash to be verified, and the certificate is not committed.** Measured first, before a line was written: it lives under a directory the repository ignores entirely, so on a fresh clone the bytes do not exist and a gate that hashed them would pass vacuously in continuous integration. That is the same defect already ruled on for the retired-doctrine corpus. The hash is verified as ATTESTED and well-formed rather than recomputed, and the reduction is an assertion in the gate rather than a footnote under it. | `git ls-files` over that tree returns zero | re-scoped and recorded, with the nonclaim naming what no gate over this repository could catch |
| **The battery is RUN, not cited.** Twenty-two structural mutations execute as part of the one command with zero false greens, and the second battery — which needs a live daemon and the untracked certificate, so it cannot run here — has its population pinned in the source that owns it, because a battery whose cases are quietly deleted is the classic way a green gate stops meaning anything. | the self-test executes 22 of 22; the durable battery's 22 cases are counted in source | both pinned, one executed |
| **The gate's own drills were built wrong twice, and both are recorded beside the code that fixes them.** The first cut asserted PREDICATES in isolation — that a malformed hash fails a regex, that an all-zero commit does not resolve — which is trivially true and silent about whether the gate would go red. The rewrite planted defects and re-ran the gate, but joined absolute override paths onto the repository root, so every evidence drill went red because the file could not be READ: each drill passed while proving nothing about the assertion it named. That is precisely the failure mode these drills exist to prevent, reproduced inside them. | eight drills now plant a defect and require a non-zero exit NAMING that assertion; a child run is red on exactly the planted assertion and green on the rest | fixed, and the override seam is separately proven to fail closed outside the battery |

### Journey verification LII (2026-09-12, MVP finish-line program, leg 0 — the first unmasked run, and the twelve reds it was finally able to show)

The masking split did what it was for: the first full run after it reported eight red jobs
where earlier runs had reported one, and repairing those uncovered four more that the
earlier red in their own gate had been standing in front of. None of the twelve was new.
Every one is recorded here with what it measured, because a run that surfaces twelve
pre-existing reds is the gate working, not the gate breaking. The last four are worth
reading together: a gate that stops at its first finding hides the rest of its own subject,
and the browser smoke stops at the first route that fails — so each repair bought exactly
one more finding, four times over.

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Three pinned censuses had drifted, each by a commit that changed the population and did not move the pin.** The daemon's walked token population moved by eighteen; the router's registered route set by six; the estate's absence-worded verifier assertions by one. Each drift is attributed to an exact commit rather than to a window: reverting one file returns the census to precisely its old pin, two commits add three route registrations each and no others touch a registration line, and the gate's own label regex differs in exactly one file between the previous basis and now. | `check:ontology-admission-census` 27/27; `check:named-gap-truth` 12/12 with 3/3 mutations red on target; CONFIRMED IN CI on run 34691442843 — both the Rust-backed verifier family, which runs the census, and Build and Test, which replays its mutation battery, are green there | re-pinned with the attribution on each pin |
| **A prebuild built a node profile nothing reads.** The test harness keys its node-binary directory by a hash of the resolved feature string, and the workflows restated that string as the four-validator profile while the fixture's default had become the single-node one — which resolves to no consensus feature at all. The prebuild filled one directory and the fixture read another, found nothing, and rebuilt four release binaries inside its twenty-minute readiness ceiling. Both directories were observed on the host. | the two directory hashes; the failing job still compiling when its window closed; CONFIRMED IN CI on run 34691442843 — the plane verifier that had failed every run for nine days across three causes is green | fixed, and the three restatements collapsed into one composite action policed by a test in the file that owns the derivation |
| **A contract convergence had a second consumer nobody enumerated.** The packages journey still authored the retired v1 surface descriptor against a daemon that refuses v1 rather than translating it, so one 422 failed eighteen findings behind it. Two identity fields compounded it: a v2 descriptor and a v2 domain app each carry an identity the v1 reader does not name, so the journey bound nothing and the admission behind it refused for a reference of its own. | `check:packages-journey` 51/51, and the journey now witnesses the v1 refusal rather than avoiding it | fixed |
| **Two surfaces hid their only path to a sibling behind having data.** A gate crashed clicking a link that renders only when an ontology is selected; a second surface was unreachable from the native shell entirely for the same reason. Neither link is data-scoped — one carries no ontology, the other no environment. Ruled: sibling-surface navigation is not data-conditional. | `check:native-shell` 118/118 | fixed in both empty states |
| **The shared ontology link helper had been dropping `embed` for every caller that followed its own documentation.** The helper filters to a closed key list, `embed` is not in it, and the module's comment has always said embed is preserved by passing it in extra — which one surface does at three call sites. Every such link navigated out of the embed. Handled explicitly rather than added to that list: the list is the vocabulary read back as the ontology SELECTION, and embed is a fact about delivery. | the manager's context links now carry the delivery mode; two planted drills red on target | fixed, and the explorer's two row builders now thread it |
| **An assertion that was neither flaky nor vacuous but unprovable.** It conjoined a form the surface always emits with row onclicks that exist only once the estate holds object types, on a gate that runs against a freshly created isolated daemon. Split so each half names the population it measured, with the row half proven at the builder and that reduction stated rather than hidden. | the split assertion with its population printed | fixed |
| **A gate failing for its environment rather than its subject, in a job whose own comment already said why.** A verifier mints a standing envelope through a signer binary, and the helper builds that binary when absent — a cargo invocation in a deliberately Node-only job, which died on a system library that job has no reason to carry. The sibling signer already rides along in the daemon artifact for exactly this reason. | `check:session-authority-profile` 23/23 once the binary is present | fixed the same way |
| **A landing hero's 3D model has never decoded in production, and the policy that blocked it was unguarded.** The hero loads a Draco-compressed model whose decoder is inlined and started from a blob URL; the server sent no worker directive at all, so it fell back to the script directive and every decoder start was blocked — four policy violations per page load, and the model silently never decoded. The worker directive is added, and it widens the worker source without widening the script source: a remote or inline script still cannot execute, and so still cannot mint the blob a worker would start from. The layer below then asked for more — compiling WebAssembly under a strict script policy needs an eval allowance — and the answer was no: the app already ships a pure-JavaScript decoder beside the WebAssembly one, and selecting it lets the hero run under the posture the server declares. One widening stands because the decoder runs in a worker either way; the second was declined. The more durable finding is that NOTHING asserted that header — it could have been widened or deleted with every gate staying green, which is how a security posture ends up edited by whoever is passing. | a test now pins the directive set as a set, each directive named beside what it is for; two planted drills red on target, 18/18 | fixed, and the posture is now policed |
| **A visual port moved a catalogue and dropped its only nonclaim, and one red carried both.** One product target's route semantics require two strings on its listing routes and both were missing. Traced exactly: the commit that set the expectation left the app carrying both, and the visual-port landing changed the app and left the gate behind. The two halves are answered differently. The landing route is no longer the catalogue at all — measured in a browser, it renders a hero and an admission ladder and never mentions a listing, while the catalogue moved to its own route — so that row had been asserting the wrong SUBJECT, and both rows now assert what their route is, with the landing asserting its onward path so that a landing which stopped reaching the catalogue still fails. The heading expectation separately carried a terminal period the port dropped, on a heading that is deliberately screen-reader-only under a recorded owner direction; that is ordinary style and the EXPECTATION is corrected rather than the product. The sentence beside it is the catalogue's nonclaim — being listed there confers nothing, the one thing about a listing that is not obvious from looking at one — and the port removed the block that carried it without carrying it over, so only a source comment still says it and no reader sees a comment. That half is restored in the product, in the slot the surrounding code already reserves for catalogue-wide statements. | the app's own suite 17/17 and its production authority scan green after the restore | the expectation corrected on one half, the product on the other |
| **Every owned surface builds the same header, and eleven of the fifteen could not survive a phone width.** The header is a flex row whose right-hand group is pushed over with `margin-left:auto` and holds chips, trays and a full-sentence primary button; the row cannot wrap, so at 390px the document scrolls sideways. FOUR of the fifteen groups already carried the wrap rule and eleven did not — which is what per-surface copies of one rule look like after a while, and why the smoke found the same overflow on a second surface the moment the first was repaired. Fixed ONCE, in the owned-surface sender, injected last in the head so it wins on document order rather than by `!important`, and scoped to phone widths so the desktop renders the pixel certifications cover are untouched. The per-surface copy written for the first instance was then removed rather than left beside it. | the smoke measures every route for horizontal overflow at 390px, which is how both instances were found | fixed at the single owner |
| **A defect fence keyed on a route label, where the product does not honour that label.** A browser-smoke abort on the vendored event stream is fenced by a full-tuple allowance whose second element asks the route table whether the final route is a vendored-SPA row. Measured: the owned route and the vendored row return the same shell — each with the SPA mount point and one bundle entry — while genuinely owned surfaces have neither. So identical behaviour by the same bundle was fatal on one route and fenced on the other. The element is replaced by a measurement of the delivered document, taken in the DOM; every other element is unchanged, and a real outage failure class stays fatal. | the fence's retained predicate tests 14/14, with the route-table test kept and rewritten to record the fact that made the old proxy unsound | fixed, recorded as a governed change to a defect fence |
| **The last masking pair in the workflow file.** Two gates shared one shell because both drive a daemon the shell's own trap reaps, so they cannot be one step per gate. Under the shell's abort-on-error the first red meant the second gate never ran at all — which is how a crash in it stayed invisible while the step was reported red for the other. | each gate now runs, each failure is remembered, and the step fails at the end | fixed |

### Journey verification LI (2026-09-12, MVP finish-line program, leg 2 — M10.5 measured, and a registry-driven gate that cannot see an absence)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **Six of roughly sixty-nine Foundry families are registered, and the two this unit names are not among them.** `FoundrySpec` and `FoundryRunPlan` have no schema file, no registry entry and no generated projection — while their daemon routes are live and free-form: the module takes untyped JSON, never calls the contract validator that 299 other call sites in this estate use, passes its inputs and steps through explicitly untyped, mints schema-version strings that appear in no registry, and persists to a filesystem record directory rather than the shared write path. Every malformed body is currently a 201. | `foundry_routes.rs`; the registry's six `Foundry*` entries | measured; the unit stays open |
| **Three of the six required fingerprint elements do not exist in any form.** Model, recipe and time-to-quality are zero-hit across the whole repository. What ships is a single composite hardware/software/topology fingerprint of eight CPU, OS and release members, with a topology that is a single CPU process by construction. A check asserting six named elements would be asserting against three that are not there. | `foundry-qualified-measurement.v1.schema.json`; the zero-hit searches | recorded |
| **The registry-driven gate is green precisely because it cannot see an absence.** `check:architecture-contracts` reads the registry and proves every registered contract's schema, fixtures and generated projections are current. It never enumerates the families canon defines and asks whether they are registered — so it passes today with sixty-three families unregistered and two free-form routes live. That is not a defect in that gate; it is the shape of the hole this unit exists to fill, and it is worth stating because a green contracts gate is easy to mistake for contract coverage. | `generate-architecture-contracts.mjs` | recorded |
| **The standing owner ruling was checked and is intact.** Canon already rules that no UI may claim the specs and run-plan families until their wire contracts are registered. The one surface that mentions them renders a link for a reference the daemon itself minted — a projection of an existing approval, not a claim of the family. The violation to fix is the free-form route, which is the unit's own subject. | the approvals surface's link renderer | no violation |

### Journey verification L (2026-09-12, MVP finish-line program, leg 2 — M08.8 measured before it was built, and what the measurement found)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The unit was measured before a check was written, and the check was not written.** Non-Negotiable 36 requires one registered compiler serving five projections over eleven independent axes. On this tree two of the eleven axes have NO FIELD anywhere — `surface_origin` and `surface_creation_method` exist only in canon prose — and two of the five projections DO NOT EXIST: the command palette is a single disabled reference-only affordance carrying its own named-gap title, and the contextual resolver's registration field is empty on all fifteen registrations with no resolver code anywhere. A check authored against the unit's text would have asserted things that are false. The unit stays a release-readiness blocker with its remaining work named, because a check that passed while two of five projections do not exist is a partial reading as a pass. | private register R-41 | measured; the unit stays open |
| **Seven registered axes were read and dropped, and that is why the compiler re-invented one of them.** The daemon's projection joined release → installation → serving, reading admission, package disposition, installation, enablement, operational state, class and availability — and emitted two of them plus one boolean and one opaque reason code. ### Journey verification XLVII (2026-09-12, MVP finish-line program, leg 2 — M06.5: the census stops describing a file set it cannot entail)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The residual was named honestly, and that is what made it closable.** The admission census's own assertion text says it is NOT rustc's file set and does not claim to be, because `mod` has no totality edge: a `#[cfg_attr(…, path)]` redirect is invisible to it and a `mod` declared inside a macro never reaches its visitor, so rustc could compile one file while the census reads another. The unit's scope said to entail the file set from what the COMPILER sees, and to NAME the boundary rather than model it if entailment were unreachable — no third hardening edge on a twice-falsified design. Entailment was reachable from the cheapest layer available: rustc already writes the exact list of files it read, per binary, as dep-info, and the check regenerates it with a build in the same run so the set it entails is this tree's. | `scripts/check-admission-census-entailment.mjs`; `target/debug/hypervisor-daemon.d` | landed |
| **Both directions, and the answer was clean.** rustc reads 118 files under the daemon binary's tree: 116 Rust modules, matching the census's walk EXACTLY in both directions, plus two JSON data includes. Those two are not filtered away for being the wrong extension — they are matched by name against the census's own production `include_str!` records, which it deliberately pins rather than follows. An unexplained file rustc read is a finding whatever it is. | the entailment clauses; 116 == 116 | landed |
| **Residual 2 is closed by closing the VOCABULARY, which is the half a size pin cannot reach.** The census pins the size of each unadjudicable bucket in both directions, so a count that moves is caught. A resolver taught to emit a seventh bucket kind would produce names no pin mentions, and every one of them would be counted by nothing. The bucket vocabulary is now derived from the resolver's own source and required to equal the pinned vocabulary both ways, with the three sharp buckets — an ambiguous module, a qualifier resolving to something unreadable, a const-of-const cycle — each pinned at zero. That is what makes "resolution is total" a property: a name either resolves or lands in a bounded, pinned place, and there is no third. | the vocabulary clauses; five drills | landed |
| **A comparison between two sets that are not the same subject, caught by its own first run.** Run 1 compared all 93 recorded includes against the binary's dep-info and went red on four `#[cfg(test)]` fixtures rustc had no reason to read. The difference was true and the comparison was wrong: dep-info describes the compilation of the BINARY, and test fixtures are compiled into the harness. Restricted to production includes, which is what a binary's dep-info can speak to. | the run, recorded beside the assertion | landed |
| **A second stranding from the same incomplete mutation battery, and this one reached a commit.** The battery re-derives the pins its mutant moves by editing the census verifier itself. When it ended without restoring, it left four pins describing a tree that contained its planted writer — family mentions, token mentions, judged positions and the family writer bucket, each exactly +1 — and those values were committed. The standing pre-commit practice that caught its Rust plant is a status check over `crates/`, and the verifier is not under `crates/`. Caught on the next census run, because the corrected tree measured one BELOW its own pins. Corrected; 27/27. Recorded for the harness's owner: a battery that edits both the subject and the gate can leave the gate pinned to a defective tree while the subject looks clean. | private register R-38b; `check:ontology-admission-census` 27/27 | corrected here, closure open for the harness owner |

### Journey verification XLVI (2026-09-12, MVP finish-line program, leg 1 — M03.8: a ceremony provisions custody, and STOPS)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The seam existed at one end only.** The daemon has CONSUMED principal-authority bindings since the portable-authority work: it resolves a principal's approval authority and verifies retained binding proofs before any grant is consumed. Nothing provisioned one. `device_custody_routes.rs` is the WebAuthn identity slice — registration, login, passkey list and revoke, the authority ceremony — and the wallet's `issue_principal_authority_binding` requires the control root's signature, so neither end reached the other. This unit is the seam and nothing else: a completed, user-verified ceremony records one immutable provisioning naming the exact portable principal, the device-held approval key, its suite, the snapshot hash the binding will freeze, and the device metadata. | `crates/node/src/bin/hypervisor_daemon_routes/device_held_principal_routes.rs`; `check:device-held-wallet-principal` | landed |
| **The daemon proposes and STOPS, and the binding state is read back rather than asserted.** Issuing the binding is the wallet control root's act; a daemon that could mint its own principal bindings would be a second authority spine. So the recorded state is `proposed`, and `GET …/binding-state` answers by calling `resolve_principal_authority` — `bound` only when wallet.network resolves that principal to the exact key this provisioning named, `diverged` when it resolves to a different one, `unresolvable` when nothing answers. A daemon reporting its own proposal as bound would be answering the question the seam exists to hand over. | the binding-state route; the clause that requires `unresolvable` with no wallet configured | landed |
| **Identity never implies provisioning, and one ceremony provisions at most one principal.** The route requires the exact `auth_factor_receipt_id` of a completed ceremony and re-reads that receipt from the identity plane's own family: it must exist, belong to this caller, be user-verified, and carry the structural non-authority claim. A ceremony recorded for `standing_effect_authority` is refused by name, because consenting to an effect is a different act from enrolling the custody that may later consent. Presenting the same receipt under a second key is refused as consumed — a receipt that could back many provisionings would turn one user-verified touch into unbounded custody. | six refusals, each by its own code, against a real virtual authenticator | landed |
| **"Authorizes nothing" is measured twice, not asserted.** Every other family the daemon serves is read before and after the admission and required byte-identical, with every watched route asserted to have answered 200 — because a route that does not exist compares equal to itself. Behind that, the module's production source is required to contain ZERO record-writer, mint, issue-binding, seal-envelope or grant-consumption CALL: an absence, which is what "holds no writer" means in code and is durable where a call count is not. The first draft pinned bare NAMES and went red on its own refusal allowlists — `standing_envelope` and `capability_lease` appear in this module precisely because it refuses them. A name is data; a call is a writer. | the estate snapshot clause; the source-absence clause; drills M2 and M3 | landed |
| **Custody is not consent, and no secret has anywhere to go.** Four consent-shaped fields and four secret-shaped fields are each refused by their OWN code rather than as unknown fields, so a caller told "unknown field" about `recovery_phrase` does not merely fail — they learn the plane stores no secret at all. Drill M4 requires an ordinary unknown field to be refused as unknown, so the two classes are aimed rather than catching everything. | eight refusals plus drill M4 | landed |
| **A principal the binding plane can never resolve is refused, not recorded.** The wallet owner's canonical grammar is five schemes with a strict segment shape. The first draft accepted any `scheme://path` and its own check then provisioned `wallet://…` — a scheme that has never existed there. Recording a provisioning for an unresolvable principal is worse than refusing it: it is a durable statement that custody was arranged when it was not. The grammar is now enforced here and four malformed references are refused by name. | `CANONICAL_PRINCIPAL_SCHEMES`; four grammar clauses | landed |
| **Standalone-local operation, corrected from a false claim to a true one.** The first draft asserted that no IdP ceremony is registered. That is simply false — `/v1/hypervisor/auth/oidc/start` and `/callback` exist — and asserting it would have been a green light for a fact that is not true. What the unit requires is narrower: a local deployment stays fully operable with NO IdP ACCOUNT. So the lane is required to be present and INERT: it resolves an admitted SSO configuration, none is admitted, it is CALLED and refuses, and the whole ceremony completes anyway. The daemon's own environ block is read to confirm no IdP variable is set. | the IdP-lane clauses; the environ measurement | landed |
| **The sixth proof is composed, not restated** — `check:passkey-only-authority` runs as a supplementary and carries forward both halves it already owns: a passkey-only account needing no plaintext credential, and its typed absence that no account-recovery route exists. Its finding that retiring the development plaintext credential is therefore irreversible is inherited unchanged; this unit authorises no deletion. | the composed clause | landed |
| **Fourteen verifiers were inheriting a fixture profile that cannot commit.** Composing that supplementary is what surfaced it: `check:passkey-only-authority` never returned on this host, and the cause was the wallet fixture's default — `parse_ordering_profile(None)` answers `Aft`, a four-validator cluster. Eleven callers pin single-node ordering explicitly; fourteen named nothing and silently got four validators. That is also the cause of the M04 plane verifiers' nine-run red. Ruled: the DEFAULT moves to single-node in the fixture library itself, with the four-validator profile available by naming it. Authority semantics are identical under either; a default that cannot commit is not a stricter default, it is an unusable one. Measured on the way: killing such a fixture strands four validators at over 100% CPU each, orphaned to init. | `wallet-network-principal-authority-fixture.mjs`; private register R-36 | landed |

Each is now one step per gate with `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}`, and steps that need the built daemon are gated on that build's own outcome rather than on the step before them. Two `if: always()` evidence uploads became `!cancelled() && steps.<smoke>.outcome != 'skipped'`: a skipped smoke made the upload fail "no files found" and added a second red that meant nothing. **Nothing is downgraded** — a red step is a red job is a red run; what changes is that a red names exactly its own gate. | `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; the run on `8315d8128` (34673348110) as the before, the run on this commit as the after | landed |

### Journey verification XLIV (2026-09-12, MVP finish-line program, leg 2b — M03.7: an externally owned account binds a product session and becomes no kind of authority)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **The verifying successor to `link_owner@v1` exists.** `link_owner@v1` stored a caller-supplied `OwnerAnchor` having verified NOTHING — not the signature, message, chain, nonce, domain, expiry or replay state — and has failed closed since W1.6 because admitting an unverified anchor is exactly the standing-access defect INV-40 forbids. Its registered replacement contracts (`wallet-authentication-challenge/v1`, `wallet-ownership-proof/v1`) had been generated into Rust types for months with no handler behind them. `handlers/wallet_siwe.rs` is that handler: `mint_wallet_authentication_challenge@v1` and `verify_wallet_ownership_proof@v1`, two operations because minting a challenge and answering one are different acts by different parties at different times — folding them into one call would make the nonce meaningless. | `crates/services/src/wallet_network/handlers/wallet_siwe.rs`; the two dispatch arms; `npm run check:wallet-siwe-link-owner-v2` **PASS 7/7 checks; 11 pinned tests** | landed |
| **The signed message is rebuilt from the STORED challenge, and the verify request carries no message field at all.** A verifier that hashed a caller-supplied message would establish that the caller signed *something*, which is not the claim. The ERC-4361 text is reconstructed from the challenge's own domain, uri, chain, nonce, account and timestamps, so "the signature is valid" and "the signature is over THIS challenge" are the same statement. A signature over any other challenge recovers a different address and is refused by the recovered identity, not by a message comparison — which is what the test asserts. | the handler's `erc4361_message`; `a_signature_over_a_different_challenge_does_not_verify_against_this_one` | landed |
| **Identity is not authority, asserted by measurement rather than by comment.** The proof and the factor both record `effect_authority_created: false`; the module holds no writer for any approval, grant, delegation, injection or policy key, which is a source pin at ZERO; and the positive test prefix-scans six authority families after a successful verification and requires every one to be empty. A consequential path is still a separate, explicitly committed authority request, exactly as before this unit existed. | the `effect_authority_created` fields; the prefix-scan assertion; source pin `approval_key\|approval_grant_state_key\|session_delegation_key\|injection_grant_key\|policy_key` hit 0 | landed |
| **Every substitution is refused by its own name, with nothing written.** Ten tests drive REAL secp256k1 signatures: a valid signature by another key (refused naming the address it actually proves), a signature over another challenge, an account on another chain (a factor is chain-qualified — the same address on two chains is two factors), an account the challenge did not target, a replayed consumed nonce, and an expired window which is recorded as `expired` rather than left looking issued. Each refusal re-reads the challenge and requires its status unchanged: a refusal that consumed the nonce would burn a challenge on a path that failed. | the ten `wallet_siwe` tests | landed |
| **ERC-1271 and ERC-6492 fail closed BEFORE the challenge is touched.** Both require reading the wallet contract's own `isValidSignature` answer at an exact chain state, and this build binds no audited on-chain verifier. The proof contract requires `contract_wallet_state_ref` to record the state observed, and a state nobody observed cannot be recorded — so they are refused by name rather than approximated. Refusing before consumption matters: refusing afterwards would burn a challenge on a path this build cannot complete. | `a_contract_account_kind_fails_closed_before_the_challenge_is_touched`; source pin on the refusal text | landed |

| **The failure class, named once.** A wrapper bounded a verifier with `spawnSync` + `killSignal: SIGKILL`, which kills the lone child; node runs no `'exit'` hook on SIGKILL (nor on an unhandled SIGTERM), so every daemon and fixture group the verifier owned outlived the bound — three validator processes for 23 h (2026-09-09), a recipe-run-split daemon for 30 h (found and reaped by PID this leg), and the sequence-zero lane itself waited nine hours at 0 % CPU because nothing inside it had a deadline (R-15). | `scripts/check-cargo-test-population.mjs` `run()` (before); `ps` of the stranded daemons | recorded |
| **Fixed as a pattern, in the three places that carry it.** The sequence-zero verifier gains typed deadlines — per journey (`IOI_SYSTEM_SEQUENCE_ZERO_JOURNEY_DEADLINE_MS`, default 180 min: named-continuity measured 59 min idle and its dissolution ladder ran past 90 min on a loaded host twice), per run (`…_RUN_DEADLINE_MS`, default journeys × 180 min + 30 min) and per synchronous child command (`…_COMMAND_DEADLINE_MS`, default 60 min — a synchronous `spawnSync` blocks the event loop, so no JavaScript deadline can reach it and each carries its own); a malformed value is refused, never defaulted. On a deadline it saves a bounded tail of every owned daemon log under `.artifacts/sequence-zero-stalls/`, reaps every owned child and process group by the identities it registered, and FAILS NAMING the journey. SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP reap the same way and exit 128+signal. Both wrappers (`check-cargo-test-population.mjs`, `check-acceptance-consumer-afternoon.mjs`) now start a bounded supplementary in its own process group and kill the GROUP on deadline (SIGTERM, 20 s grace, SIGKILL) and once more after any exit, and pass their own signals through. Nothing is killed by pattern anywhere. | the three scripts; `--mutation` self-drill 3/3 on the patched wrapper; module import smoke + malformed-deadline refusal | landed |

| **R-21 ruled and closed — the pure function was the wrong side.** `d33965a9f` (2026-08-24, "normalize live MCP transport") added `if normalized.contains("__") { extension.invoke }` — a capability by NAME SHAPE — which swept the wallet's mail-connector SETUP tools (`wallet_network__mail_connector_upsert` and its `_get` / `_ensure_binding` siblings) past the 2026-02-27 subtraction (`57bf8f71f`, "block mail connector setup tools"). The doctrine is subtraction: the estate's sibling test `unregistered_prefixed_tools_do_not_gain_capabilities_by_name_shape` already says name shape confers nothing, and a setup tool is a configuration act admitted by the wallet's own connector-config surface, not a conversational tool. Fix: a tool in the estate's own `wallet_network__` namespace carries only the capabilities an explicit binding declares (the mail tools through their explicit registry bindings, e.g. `wallet_network__mail_read_latest` → `mail.read.latest`; their alias names through the `conversation.reply` fallback); the name-shape arm stays for live extension (MCP) tools outside every estate namespace, which `maybe_typed_runtime_mcp_tool_call` depends on. A new test pins BOTH directions so the subtraction cannot over-reach. Canon: `connectors-tools/doctrine.md` § *Capability Tiering*. | `cargo test --locked -p ioi-services --lib`: **3625 passed; 0 failed; 4 ignored (244.8 s)** (was 3623 passed / 1 failed on `6bc07025d`); `wallet_namespace_tools_gain_no_capability_by_name_shape_but_extension_tools_keep_theirs` | landed |
| **The floor.** A new CI job, `rust_library_suite_floor`, runs the library suite of every crate measured green plus `ioi-services`, one `cargo test --locked -p <crate> --lib` per line with the measured tests · compile s · test s · wall s beside it, so the bind is honest about cost. Budget, stated: the job's 90-minute ceiling (the sibling jobs'); measured 2182 s (36.4 min) for the floored lines on a warm target — the whole floor fits, nothing is left out for cost. Its own job so it never pushes `build_and_test` past its ceiling and a red names the crate. Not floored, each a typed absence with a closure test: `ioi-node`'s ten non-daemon bins, `aft-c8-verifier` and `ioi-ontology-census` (no lib target; closure: `--bins` measured and floored), and every crate's integration-test targets (`--tests`; closure: a second measurement, floored where green). `ioi-types`/`ioi-api`/`ioi-pii`/`ioi-telemetry` already run in `build_and_test` under `--all-features`. | `.github/workflows/ci.yml` `rust_library_suite_floor`; the evidence file's `ci_floor` and `typed_absences` | landed (CI proves it on the next push) |

### Journey verification XXXIV (2026-09-10, correctness program before the review, leg 1 — the connector authority plane speaks one language: a registration binds its principal and is never overwritten, and the four authority routes refuse an unresolved caller identically)

| Delta | Evidence | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **R-20 ruled and implemented — a connector's identity binds the principal that registered it.** `handle_connector_register` took no `HeaderMap` and derived `connector_id` from `{service}:{name}:{base_url}` alone, so a second caller presenting the same triple overwrote the first caller's record — `org_policy.principal_scoped` included — which is the scoping R-17 built bind and revoke on. It now resolves its caller with `require_authenticated_principal` as its FIRST statement, records the holder on the record (`owner_ref: user://<principal_id>`, server-resolved, never from the body), and refuses an EXISTING id with a typed `409 connector_already_registered` for every caller, the holder included. Built as a HOLDER CHECK, not a principal in the id: session profiles name `connector:<id>` as a closed set and lease grants, standing leases and credentials join on the id, so re-keying by principal would orphan every existing record, and the ruling forbids a silent re-key; the holder's own re-register is refused too because it would rewrite its standing lease, admitted policy and credential posture in place (widening is a new binding on record). Records that predate the ruling carry no holder: never backfilled (that would attribute a registration to a principal who did not perform it), never re-keyed, refused on re-registration like any other, and read by nothing — bind, revoke and invoke keep R-17's single ownership notion. Canon: ADR 0052 § 8; `connectors-tools/doctrine.md` § *Connector Authority*; `core-clients-surfaces.md` § *Session authority profile*. | `check:admission-evidence` **green, exit 0, H-census 201/201** — `handle_connector_register` LEFT the rule-H baseline (entry deleted, not re-pinned) · `check:standing-consumer-loop` **56/56** (was 45/45; +11) — the drill registers under the operator and reads `owner_ref` back from disk; a REAL second principal (created through the route, logged in, resolving as itself) re-registers the same triple → 409 and the record is untouched (`owner_ref` the operator's, `principal_scoped` still true), then cannot bind (404), revoke (404) or draw (403 `principal_not_authorized`); the holder's own retry → 409 with `allowed_tools` unchanged; a different triple → 200 new id; a record rewritten without `owner_ref` → 409 by id, bytes unchanged, projection without `owner_ref` | landed |
| **R-22 ruled and implemented — register, bind, revoke and invoke speak one language to an unresolved caller.** After R-17, bind and revoke refused an anonymous caller while invoke attributed it to the literal `"local-operator"` and refused only when the connector was principal-scoped: an anonymous caller could DRAW an envelope it could neither mint nor revoke, and revoke is the safety act. All four now answer `401 hypervisor.authentication_required`, byte-identical, before any record read; invoke's literal and its unreachable `principal_required` arm are gone; the act tool, which delegates to invoke under the caller's headers, inherits the refusal (an anonymous handle-only act drew as the local operator before). **Posture, recorded either way:** `auth_gate` defaults to `auto` (enforce only when the daemon is exposed or the request is forwarded), the served App stamps `x-ioi-forwarded` only for a non-loopback browser, and bootstrap sets no policy — the shipped alpha enables enforcement NOWHERE, so in-handler resolution is the entire gate in the posture the alpha ships, and every verifier boots its daemon the same way. Authenticated callers are unchanged on all four routes: the session-scoped path still resolves the session's write owner after the caller resolves (the anonymous session-scoped 401 keeps its status; its code moves from `request_principal_required` to the shared one), and the App's run lane crosses under the operator's session (`ioi-api-adapter.mjs` forwards cookie/authorization on every daemon call). Nonclaim: the daemon's internal dispatch token resolves no principal and never invoked a connector; such a call now refuses typed instead of being attributed to the local operator. | `check:session-authority-profile` **23/23** (was 22/22; +1) — the CI-bound four-route assertion; floor re-pinned 22 → 23 (`verifier-floors.v1.json`, names sha256 `cb1a9ce6…` → `c519a216…`) · `check:standing-consumer-loop` 56/56 — unresolved caller on all four routes byte-identical, invoke's refusal preceding the record read (existing and made-up ids answer identically), anonymous headless act 401 · `check:named-gap-truth` 12/12 + 3/3 mutations (absence-label pin unmoved) · alpha journey, fixture mode: 33/33 (evidence copied to `_meta/evidence/m13-alpha-journey-fixture-connector-authority-2026-09-10.v1.json`) · `cargo test --locked -p ioi-node --bin hypervisor-daemon`: 1148 passed, 0 failed, 8 ignored (118 s) | landed |
| **Recorded, not resolved — six unbound connector scripts register anonymously against the shared daemon** (`verify-hypervisor-connector-execution`, `-app-parity-lineage`, `-app-parity-studio-designer`, `-governed-build`, `-provenance-proof-stream-threading`, `-semantic-journey`): none is package- or CI-bound, `lib/isolated-daemon.mjs` already names them as legacy debt, and their first register now answers 401 instead of writing into the real registry. Closure test: each boots an isolated plane and bootstraps a session. `check:connector-estate`, which this program was told to re-run, does not exist in either package.json; no connector verifier is package-bound. | `apps/hypervisor/scripts/lib/isolated-daemon.mjs` header; `grep` of both package files | open — verifier debt |
| **Two facts this program was handed, corrected at source.** CI runs the full suite of SIX crates, not two: `ioi-types`, `ioi-api`, `ioi-pii`, `ioi-telemetry` run under `--all-features` at ci.yml:187-191 beside the daemon binary and `agentgres`. And `cargo fmt --all -- --check` (ci.yml:79-83) is red on `6bc07025d` under the pinned toolchain's rustfmt 1.8.0 in six daemon files this leg did not touch (`cloud_job_routes.rs`, `governed_authority.rs`, `ioi_intelligence_routes.rs`, `release_change_plan_routes.rs`, `runpod_candidate_source.rs`, `hypervisor-daemon.rs`) and `crates/cli/src/bin/wallet_network_local_authority.rs`; the four hunks in `lifecycle_routes.rs` (three from R-17/R-19, one this leg's) are formatted here.
